./main_3/README.md:1:# `main_3`: Corrosion Degradation and Proxy-RUL Pipeline
./main_3/README.md:3:`main_3` is the final implementation for the ferrocement corrosion project. It reconstructs the dataset, extracts classical corrosion features from images, models surface corrosion and hidden damage, fits specimen degradation curves, and estimates proxy Remaining Useful Life (RUL) from engineering limit states.

This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Scoring uses held-out terminal images, rather than a fixed early horizon. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

./main_3/reports/meeting_report/results_analysis.md:141:`main_3` successfully transforms the project from a single corrosion-regression baseline into an interpretable condition-assessment pipeline, with strong evidence for surface-corrosion modeling and only preliminary evidence for hidden-damage and proxy-RUL estimation.
./main_3/reports/meeting_report/talking_points.md:7:> `main_2` established an image-based baseline for predicting current peak corrosion. `main_3` extends that idea into a full engineering pipeline: canonical dataset construction, interpretable corrosion feature extraction, hidden-damage estimation, degradation modeling, and threshold-based proxy-RUL.
./main_3/reports/meeting_report/talking_points.md:15:- `main_3` best peak-corrosion model: **MAE 4.21**, **R2 0.784**.

./main_3/reports/meeting_report/talking_points.md:24:- That is why `main_3` is strongest on corrosion-state modeling and only moderate on hidden-damage inference.

./main_3/reports/meeting_report/main2_vs_main3.md:59:## 5. Where `main_3` is still limited
./main_3/reports/meeting_report/main2_vs_main3.md:61:`main_3` is clearly better than `main_2`, but the improvement is uneven across stages.
./main_3/reports/meeting_report/main2_vs_main3.md:78:> `main_3` is a strong improvement in system scope and corrosion-state modeling, while hidden-damage and proxy-RUL remain promising but preliminary because the structural label regime is still sparse.
./main_3/reports/meeting_report/main2_vs_main3.md:84:> `main_2` asked whether a model can read current peak corrosion from an image. `main_3` asks whether the full experimental record can be turned into a condition-assessment pipeline that estimates visible corrosion, infers hidden damage, fits degradation over time, and outputs a threshold-based proxy-RUL. The answer is yes for corrosion and partly yes for hidden damage and proxy-RUL, with clear evidence that structural supervision is now the main research bottleneck.

./main_3/reports/meeting_report/executive_summary.md:15:`main_2` implemented a specimen-grouped deep baseline for predicting **peak surface corrosion** from images. `main_3` turns the project into a broader engineering pipeline: it reconstructs a canonical time-series dataset, extracts interpretable corrosion features from images, estimates hidden damage, fits specimen-level degradation curves, and converts those curves into **proxy Remaining Useful Life (proxy-RUL)** through engineering thresholds.
./main_3/reports/meeting_report/executive_summary.md:22:- `main_3` improved the same grouped peak-corrosion task to **MAE 4.21**, **RMSE 6.23**, **R2 0.784** using hand-crafted image features plus classical models.
./main_3/reports/meeting_report/executive_summary.md:23:- `main_3` also added a new surface-corrosion task: `surface_total_rust_pct` reached **MAE 1.38** and **R2 0.646** with a random forest.

""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

This limits confirmatory interpretation, especially for small differences. A per-regime best model also differs from a fixed-model robustness test: ratios across evaluation regimes are meaningful as model deterioration only when the same model is retained. Finally, negative test $R^2$ means error exceeds the test-set-mean reference under that score. In small, narrow-range groups, this score can be highly variable; it does not imply every prediction lacks useful rank information."

The threshold is a pragmatic sensitivity cutoff. The code retains all later observations once onset occurs; it does not require each later value to remain above the cutoff."

""main_4/outputs/ultimate_load_refocus/mesh_stratified/mesh_feature_set_comparison.csv""]}","An accurate visual corrosion model need not provide a transferable estimate of structural capacity. This study examines that boundary using saved experiments on 48 ferrocement specimens from two campaigns. The aligned photographic record contains 791 observations, while wire-area loss and ultimate load are each measured once per specimen at terminal testing. Campaign identity coincides with mesh configuration, chloride concentration, and terminal exposure. A focused terminal-load comparison uses specimen-disjoint, repeated five-fold evaluation and image-only, metadata-only, and combined feature families. Scoring uses held-out terminal images, rather than a fixed early horizon. Metadata-only Ridge achieves mean terminal-test MAE of 0.173\,kN with fold standard deviation 0.046\,kN. The tested image descriptors do not establish a reliable pooled improvement. A post-onset subset yields a limited MAE gain for a combined model without uniformly better rank correlation. Every selected feature-family model has negative mean test $R^2$ within each mesh group. Campaign exclusion provides a severe extrapolation stress test rather than a representative estimate of deployment error. The contribution is a bounded empirical account of how supervision, metadata controls, and evaluation regime constrain structural claims from images. Model-derived threshold analysis is not validated remaining-life prediction, and no performance result exists for the separate prepared four-class classifier."

./CLAUDE.md:7:This is a ferrocement corrosion research project that predicts structural hidden damage and remaining useful life (RUL) from time-series surface images of corroding specimens. The repo contains four generations of the pipeline (`main`, `main_2`, `main_3`, `main_4`). **`main_4` is the current and canonical implementation.** `main_4/CODEBASE_GUIDE.md` is the authoritative reference for the architecture.

./CLAUDE.md:86:| `main_3/` | Phase 3: full structured pipeline with tests, numbered scripts in `scripts/`, parquet outputs |
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:5:**Audit method:** Five parallel deep-dive investigations (main_first+main, main_2, main_3, main_4+main_4_old, out+augmentation+Data+Documentation) plus direct verification of git history, file mtimes, config integrity, workbook contents, and several specific numeric claims by the coordinating session (a revision pass performed after the first audit draft, prompted by inconsistencies the user asked to have re-checked). Every claim below is tagged by evidence tier per the brief's authority hierarchy:
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:12:The repository's scientific/implementation work is organized as **six phases**: an informal exploratory prototype (`main_first`), a benchmarked classical-ML baseline (`main`), a deep-image-embedding experiment (`main_2`), an interpretable-feature pipeline linking corrosion to structural condition and proxy-RUL (`main_3`), a robustness-diagnostic-refinement-then-refocus phase (`main_4`, itself two sub-phases: 4a diagnostic refinement, 4b terminal ultimate-load refocus), and a later, methodologically separate four-class visible-corrosion-classification/augmentation effort (`augmentation/` + `Documentation/augmentation_*`). **`out/` is not a research phase** — it is a dissemination layer of condensed IEEE-style papers that repackage main_3/main_4 results (not independent analysis, with one apparent-but-resolved exception — see §4). `Data/` and `Documentation/` hold the master workbook/images plus a large set of project-generated planning documents, and — critically — **two genuine external sources, evidence category P (predecessor/external documentation)**: an earlier MSc thesis by a different student (Al Amin Hossain, s313940) and a 2025 conference paper (ARTISTE), both describing the original physical experimental campaigns that this project builds on and partially reproduces. These are cited where they establish facts about the physical experiment (e.g. the definition of `ultimate_load_kn`, see §4/§8) but are never treated as this project's own output.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:14:Git history is **not usable for fine-grained chronology**: the entire repo (all `main_*` generations) landed in one commit, `4ba4a65 "Add project files"` (2026-04-08), preceded only by two generic init commits. The `augmentation/` work is the only part with organic incremental commits (`f0a3253` 2026-07-06, `e1e88fb` 2026-09-10). File mtimes are more informative but must be used cautiously: a cluster of files across *every* generation (`main_first`, `main`, `main_2`, `main_3`, `main_4`, `main_4_old`) share the identical modification timestamp 2026-04-01 16:58. **Historically**, the pipeline's configuration files held valid numeric values — this is evidenced by successful, dated output artifacts that could only have been produced with working numeric config (e.g. `main_4/outputs/features/image_features.csv`, dated 2026-03-16; `run_ultimate_load_refocus.log`, dated 2026-03-25). **Currently**, four YAML config files in `main_4`/`main_4_old` contain a corruption: every literal numeral `1` inside a YAML value has been replaced with the string `main_first` (e.g. `reg_lambda: 1.0` → `reg_lambda: main_first.0`), which would break re-execution of the affected code paths today. The repo's squashed git history contains no pre-corruption diff, so the **mechanism is inferred, not proven**: the coincidence in timing with the cross-generation 2026-04-01 16:58 touch is circumstantial, consistent with (but not proof of) a single automated bulk text-replacement operation. This is treated strictly as a Software-Engineering/Reproducibility finding (§ Ch.7) and flagged as an open question in §8 — it is not used as evidence for any scientific conclusion.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:16:Dataset-size handling differs by generation, and the difference is a genuine methodological choice rather than a simple discrepancy (verified directly against each generation's loading code and saved run artifacts — see §4 item 2 for full detail): **`main`, `main_2`, and `main_3` each retain all 792 image-linked rows**, encountering the one corrupted, unreadable image (`E01-20240508-17W.png`) only at the feature/embedding-extraction step and imputing its missing values with the per-column median (verified in `main/image_features.py`, `main_2/data.py`, `main_3/src/features/extractor.py`). **`main_4` instead applies an explicit data-quality policy** (`configs/dataset.yaml: data_policy.use_only_aligned_readable_images: true`) that excludes any image it cannot align to a readable, usable row, yielding **791 aligned rows** from **792 image files** (`main_4/outputs/audit/verified_facts.json`); the later `augmentation/` effort inherits main_4's 791-row exclusion convention. In all cases there are **48 unique specimens**, of which only the **48 terminal-stage rows** carry structural labels (`wire_area_loss_frac`, `ultimate_load_kn`) — one per specimen.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:18:The scientific arc **is** evidence-supported, with important corrections to the brief's proposed story (see §7): main_3 already implemented GroupShuffleSplit/LOTO/LOCO and already *named* campaign confounding and metadata-shortcut learning in its own report — main_4 did not discover these problems, it **quantified and acted on** problems main_3 had already flagged (a nuance the final report must get right). The single strongest, most defensible finding in the whole project is main_4's ultimate-load-refocus mesh-stratification result: a pooled corrosion-vs-load correlation (Spearman ρ=0.462, p≈0.001) **weakens or reverses** once stratified by steel-mesh family (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40) — evidence of substantial confounding by specimen-group structure that prevents the pooled correlation from being read as a direct corrosion-capacity relationship, and the closest thing the project has to a decisive negative-result-turned-insight. This finding concerns the **structural** endpoint specifically; the **visible-corrosion** stage, by contrast, remains robust under the same leave-one-campaign-out evaluation (Spearman ≥0.97 for both surface targets, see §4/§7) — the collapse is specific to hidden structural condition, not a general failure of campaign generalization.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:31:| 3. ./REPORT_PLANNING.md:51:4. **Phase 3 — Interpretable corrosion → structural condition → proxy-RUL (`main_3`).** *Research question:* using genuinely interpretable, multi-family handcrafted features (91 features: color/HSV/Lab, GLCM/LBP texture, morphology, spatial/strip-position) and specimen-grouped, treatment-grouped, and campaign-grouped splits, can visible corrosion be linked to hidden structural condition and a proxy-RUL? *Result:* surface corrosion regression works well in-distribution (MAE 1.38 for total rust); hidden-damage regression is weak (`wire_area_loss_pct` R²=0.31, `ultimate_load_kn` R²=0.385, RandomForest MAE 0.121 kN on this generation's own 38/10 grouped-holdout split) and **already visibly unstable under LOTO** (R² swinging from +0.76 to −50.75 across treatment folds); the project's own report already names campaign confounding and a "downstream label shortcut" (manual corrosion labels used as damage-model inputs) as concrete technical risks; correlation between visible corrosion and wire loss is near-zero (r=0.021). Proxy-RUL is computed and consistently never mislabeled as true RUL. *Limitation discovered (explicit, in the project's own words):* "Small structural supervision… is the dominant bottleneck"; hidden-damage predictions used to build degradation curves are in-sample fits, not held-out evidence. *Why the next phase started:* main_3 itself specifies almost the entire main_4 agenda in writing (ablations isolating metadata vs. image signal, out-of-fold estimates, monotonic degradation constraints) — main_4 operationalizes fixes for problems main_3 already named, rather than discovering new ones.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:57:*(Dissemination, not a research phase)* — `out/` repackages main_3's and main_4's already-saved results into IEEE-conference format for external communication. One number initially flagged as a possible mismatch (`corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.pdf`'s "0.121 kN" ultimate-load MAE) was traced directly to main_3's own `damage_regression_metrics.csv` (RandomForest, group_shuffle split, n=38/10: MAE=0.1207) — it is main_3's own valid, version-specific result, not an error or a mismatch against main_4 (which used a different pipeline, feature set, and split composition to obtain 0.174 kN). See §4.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:65:**Note on an apparent discrepancy that did not survive verification:** an earlier draft of this register listed a mismatch between `out/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.pdf`'s "0.121 kN" ultimate-load MAE and main_4's saved "0.174 kN." Direct verification shows these are **not in conflict**: the paper (sourced from `main_3/reports/corrosion_condition_pipeline_ieee.tex`, §"Hidden-Damage Modeling") explicitly and correctly reports **main_3's own** grouped-holdout result — confirmed byte-for-byte against `main_3/outputs/damage_regression_metrics.csv` (`group_shuffle,fold_00,mixed_specimens,random_forest,ultimate_load_kn,...,38,10,0.12068...`, i.e. n_train=38/n_test=10). Main_4's 0.174 kN comes from a different generation's pipeline (different feature engineering, different code, a different — though also nominally "group_shuffle" — train/test fold composition, and an N=791 vs. main_3's N=792 basis). Both numbers are genuine, valid, version-specific historical results for the same target from two different implementations; neither is stale or in error. This is **not listed as a contradiction below** — it is recorded here only so the resolution is not lost.

./REPORT_PLANNING.md:176:This project began as an attempt to predict a ferrocement specimen's visible corrosion state and its relationship to internal structural condition directly from time-series surface photographs, progressing from a brief, informal single-algorithm prototype (`main_first`) through an increasingly rigorous classical-ML baseline (`main`) and a deep-embedding experiment (`main_2`) to a structured, interpretable pipeline (`main_3`) that explicitly attempted to connect visible corrosion to hidden structural condition and a proxy remaining-useful-life. At every phase after the first, the project's own generated reports were unusually candid about the resulting weaknesses: `main`'s handcrafted-feature model was shown to be substantially a restatement of the very color threshold used to compute its own label; `main_2`'s deep-embedding fusion underperformed a simpler image-only model on held-out data; and `main_3`, while already implementing rigorous specimen-grouped, treatment-grouped, and campaign-grouped evaluation, found that hidden-damage prediction was unstable to the point of negative R² under leave-one-treatment-out, and explicitly named the two root causes in writing: only 48 terminal-stage structural measurements exist across the entire dataset (one per specimen, with no repeated structural sampling), and the experimental campaigns are themselves confounded — steel-mesh count, NaCl concentration, and exposure duration co-vary with campaign, so a model trained within one campaign extrapolates, rather than genuinely generalizes, when applied to the other.
./REPORT_PLANNING.md:178:`main_4` took these self-diagnosed weaknesses and quantified them rigorously, and the quantification revealed something specific: it is the **structural** targets (`wire_area_loss_frac`, `ultimate_load_kn`) that collapse under leave-one-campaign-out evaluation — ultimate-load MAE inflated 3.24× relative to a standard grouped holdout — while the **visible-corrosion** targets (`surface_total_rust_pct`, `peak_rust_pct`) retain Spearman correlations at or above 0.97 under the same campaign-holdout evaluation. Feature-importance and robustness analysis further showed that whatever robust structural signal existed came predominantly from experimental-design metadata (mesh count, treatment, NaCl level) rather than from the corrosion images. Rather than treating the structural collapse as a modeling problem to be engineered around, the project made a defensible scientific decision to refocus: instead of trying to predict a sparse, campaign-confounded hidden-damage proxy or an unreachable "true" remaining-useful-life, it re-targeted the one directly measured, physically meaningful terminal endpoint available — the terminal ultimate load recorded at the end of each specimen's aging period (defined, per the predecessor thesis, as the peak load recorded during a four-point bending test) — and explicitly tested, rather than assumed, whether visible corrosion adds predictive value once specimen design is controlled for. The resulting analysis is the project's most scientifically informative finding: a pooled correlation between visible corrosion and terminal ultimate load that looks statistically real (ρ=0.462) weakens or reverses once the data is stratified by steel-mesh family (mesh_4: ρ=−0.046; mesh_7: ρ=−0.40). This indicates substantial confounding by specimen-group structure and prevents the pooled correlation from being interpreted as evidence of a direct corrosion-capacity relationship — it does not, on its own, prove the pooled relationship is purely an artifact with no underlying corrosion effect, only that the pooled evidence cannot support such a claim. This is precisely the failure mode the project had been warning about in its own documentation since main_3.
